# Plateau 12: context apparatus

Generated from `~/Downloads/protevi-response/work/context/` (do not edit by hand). Background sources for each variorum row, matched by Minnesota page. The green glosses in Column 4 explain words; this file points to where an argument is worked through elsewhere. Rows not yet written are listed in the master, `context_12_13.md`.

## 12¶1 (Minnesota 351-352)
- **Later version** (ATP 351-352). Two years after the plateau, Guattari moves the war machine into his schizoanalytic cartography as one of four positions. “La machine de guerre, c’est le fait qu’un territoire fonctionne comme machine par le détour de rapports a-signifiants et de points-signes machiniques sans rapport de signification” ("The war machine is the fact that a territory functions as a machine by way of a-signifying relations and machinic point-signs without a relation of signification"). Its opposite: “un territoire qui fonctionne avec un univers en empruntant le rapport de signification est une machine de pouvoir” ("a territory that functions with a universe by borrowing the relation of signification is a machine of power"). *Source:* `guattari-seminars/1982-01-26_26-01-1982-les-formations-du-noyau-dagencement.txt`, Guattari's seminar, 26 Jan 1982, « Les formations du noyau d'agencement », PDF 7.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 351-361). Propositions I-III in a single paragraph, three years before the plateau: “Like Felix, we assume that the war-machine has a nature and origin quite different from that of the apparatus of the State.” The nomads, arithmetic organization, and open space are already there, and minor science too: “even when the war-machine is related to a geometry, it is a quite different geometry, a sort of Archimedean geometry, a geometry of 'problems', and not of 'theorems' like Euclid's”. Becomings and the secret: “cf. the secret as the invention of the war-machine, as opposed to the 'publicity' of the despot or the man of the State”. Dumézil's eccentric warrior, de Heusch's war machine « from outside », and Clastres's war against the State all appear, with « Genghis Khan and the emperor of China » and Moses's organization of flight. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 141-42 (PDF 156-57).
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Source** (ATP 352). The warrior who « unbinds » of plateau 12 (n. 4, 352): the god of the men's societies figures « as the god who unfetters » Varuna's “(legally) bound victims; for the warrior and the sorcerer alike or, on another level, the soldier and the policeman, make inroads when necessary on the life and liberty of their fellow man”, each by procedures the other finds repugnant. The soldier and the policeman, already here, prepare the two violences of 448. *Source:* `capture/dumezil_mitra_varuna.txt`, Mitra-Varuna, PDF 108.

## 12¶3 (Minnesota 352-353)
- **Later version** (ATP 351-352). Two years after the plateau, Guattari moves the war machine into his schizoanalytic cartography as one of four positions. “La machine de guerre, c’est le fait qu’un territoire fonctionne comme machine par le détour de rapports a-signifiants et de points-signes machiniques sans rapport de signification” ("The war machine is the fact that a territory functions as a machine by way of a-signifying relations and machinic point-signs without a relation of signification"). Its opposite: “un territoire qui fonctionne avec un univers en empruntant le rapport de signification est une machine de pouvoir” ("a territory that functions with a universe by borrowing the relation of signification is a machine of power"). *Source:* `guattari-seminars/1982-01-26_26-01-1982-les-formations-du-noyau-dagencement.txt`, Guattari's seminar, 26 Jan 1982, « Les formations du noyau d'agencement », PDF 7.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 351-361). Propositions I-III in a single paragraph, three years before the plateau: “Like Felix, we assume that the war-machine has a nature and origin quite different from that of the apparatus of the State.” The nomads, arithmetic organization, and open space are already there, and minor science too: “even when the war-machine is related to a geometry, it is a quite different geometry, a sort of Archimedean geometry, a geometry of 'problems', and not of 'theorems' like Euclid's”. Becomings and the secret: “cf. the secret as the invention of the war-machine, as opposed to the 'publicity' of the despot or the man of the State”. Dumézil's eccentric warrior, de Heusch's war machine « from outside », and Clastres's war against the State all appear, with « Genghis Khan and the emperor of China » and Moses's organization of flight. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 141-42 (PDF 156-57).
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Source** (ATP 352). The warrior who « unbinds » of plateau 12 (n. 4, 352): the god of the men's societies figures « as the god who unfetters » Varuna's “(legally) bound victims; for the warrior and the sorcerer alike or, on another level, the soldier and the policeman, make inroads when necessary on the life and liberty of their fellow man”, each by procedures the other finds repugnant. The soldier and the policeman, already here, prepare the two violences of 448. *Source:* `capture/dumezil_mitra_varuna.txt`, Mitra-Varuna, PDF 108.
- **Develops** (ATP 353-354). De Heusch's public Nkongolo and secret Mbidi (n. 6) become a principle: in the archaic empire « the despot eats in public, sleeps in public ». “The secret is born with the war machine. No war machine, no secret.” The despot's mask is for showing: “It is the public mask, not the mask of the secret.” The State secret is late, from the moment States appropriate war machines. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 32-33.

- **Earlier version** (ATP 351-361). Propositions I-III in a single paragraph, three years before the plateau: “Like Felix, we assume that the war-machine has a nature and origin quite different from that of the apparatus of the State.” The nomads, arithmetic organization, and open space are already there, and minor science too: “even when the war-machine is related to a geometry, it is a quite different geometry, a sort of Archimedean geometry, a geometry of 'problems', and not of 'theorems' like Euclid's”. Dumézil's eccentric warrior, de Heusch's war machine « from outside », and Clastres's war against the State all appear, with « Genghis Khan and the emperor of China » and Moses's organization of flight. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 141-42 (PDF 156-57).

- **Develops** (ATP 353-354). De Heusch's public Nkongolo and secret Mbidi (n. 6) become a principle: in the archaic empire « the despot eats in public, sleeps in public ». “The secret is born with the war machine. No war machine, no secret.” The despot's mask is for showing: “It is the public mask, not the mask of the secret.” The State secret is late, from the moment States appropriate war machines. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 32-33.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 355-356). « Goethe et Hegel, penseurs d'Etat » against Kleist is first a matter of time and the subject: “I tried to oppose Kleist to Goethe; Kleist, subject formation, he could care less [se fout] completely. It's not his affair, his affair is a story of speeds and slownesses.” It comes in a discussion of pulsed and non-pulsed time (Boulez), which ties Kleist's catatonias and *précipitations* to smooth time. *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-II-Two-Forms-of-Segm/ATP-II-03-May-8-1977-English.txt`, seminar ATP II, 8 May 1977, PDF 9.

- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 355-356). « Goethe et Hegel, penseurs d'Etat » against Kleist is first a matter of time and the subject: “I tried to oppose Kleist to Goethe; Kleist, subject formation, he could care less [se fout] completely. It's not his affair, his affair is a story of speeds and slownesses.” It comes in a discussion of pulsed and non-pulsed time (Boulez), which ties Kleist's catatonias and *précipitations* to smooth time. *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-II-Two-Forms-of-Segm/ATP-II-03-May-8-1977-English.txt`, seminar ATP II, 8 May 1977, PDF 9.
- **Source** (ATP 357-358). The chief « who has no other instituted weapon than his prestige » (357): “this obligation to give, to which the chief is bound” is felt by the Indians as “a kind of right to subject him to a continuous looting”. If he checks the flow of gifts, he loses all prestige. *Source:* `core/clastres_society_against_the_state.txt`, Clastres, Society against the State, ch. 2 (PDF 31).
- **Develops** (ATP 357-359). Clastres's two mechanisms (chieftainship, war) are joined to Mauss's gift, and the problem Proposition II leaves open is put first: “one must have a presentiment, even if obscure – I’m not saying a clear idea – of what one wards away” Mauss's potlatch: “it is like they are a means of warding off what? It’s like a means of warding off the accumulation of wealth.” Hence “necessarily, mechanisms of anticipation – warding off” [anticipation – conjuration]. This is the root of Proposition XI's « conjurer, c'est aussi anticiper ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 49-51.

- **Earlier version** (ATP 351-361). Dumézil's eccentric warrior, de Heusch's war machine « from outside », and Clastres's war against the State all appear, with « Genghis Khan and the emperor of China » and Moses's organization of flight. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 141-42 (PDF 156-57).
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Source** (ATP 357-358). The chief « who has no other instituted weapon than his prestige » (357): “this obligation to give, to which the chief is bound” is felt by the Indians as “a kind of right to subject him to a continuous looting”. If he checks the flow of gifts, he loses all prestige. *Source:* `core/clastres_society_against_the_state.txt`, Clastres, Society against the State, ch. 2 (PDF 31).
- **Develops** (ATP 357-359). Clastres's two mechanisms (chieftainship, war) are joined to Mauss's gift, and the problem Proposition II leaves open is put first: “one must have a presentiment, even if obscure – I’m not saying a clear idea – of what one wards away” Mauss's potlatch: “it is like they are a means of warding off what? It’s like a means of warding off the accumulation of wealth.” Hence “necessarily, mechanisms of anticipation – warding off” [anticipation – conjuration]. This is the root of Proposition XI's « conjurer, c'est aussi anticiper ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 49-51.
- **Develops** (ATP 358). The bands of Bogotá and the « groupes de mondanité » are folded into one typology of anticipation–warding off: “There are certain pages of Clastres that absolutely chime with descriptions of bands.” Primitive societies, bands of boys, and worldly society « all go together ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 51-52.
- **Develops** (ATP 359). Why the State cannot come from war (359, 427): “everything that archaeology reveals to us about the most ancient State apparatuses, seems to confirm that they did not dispose of an army”. They had bureaucracy, police, prisons. “In order for the war machine to be integrated into the State apparatus, there already has to be a State apparatus.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 13.
- **Develops** (ATP 359-360). Why Clastres stayed an evolutionist (359-60): “it is not by introducing a rupture that one breaks evolution. At most one introduces a mutation into evolution”. On his late turn to Tupi-Guarani prophetism (n. 15), Deleuze is blunter than the note: a « slightly bizarre explanation », “invoking prophetism in order to take account of the State apparatus”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 52-53.

- **Develops** (ATP 361-362). The plateau's sentence on figures and « the affections that happen to them » is worked through as a Euclid-Archimedes opposition: “What is the difference between a problem and a theorem? It's that a problem is not of the essence type; it is of the event type, something happening, or of the operation type.” The operations are listed as “an ablation, an addition, a squaring, a cubing (cubature), a whole surgery of the figure”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 194.

The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Develops** (ATP 361-362). The plateau's sentence on figures and « the affections that happen to them » is worked through as a Euclid-Archimedes opposition: “What is the difference between a problem and a theorem? It's that a problem is not of the essence type; it is of the event type, something happening, or of the operation type.” The operations are listed as “an ablation, an addition, a squaring, a cubing (cubature), a whole surgery of the figure”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 194.

## 12¶24 (Minnesota 361-362)
- **Earlier version** (ATP 351-361). Propositions I-III in a single paragraph, three years before the plateau: “Like Felix, we assume that the war-machine has a nature and origin quite different from that of the apparatus of the State.” The nomads, arithmetic organization, and open space are already there, and minor science too: “even when the war-machine is related to a geometry, it is a quite different geometry, a sort of Archimedean geometry, a geometry of 'problems', and not of 'theorems' like Euclid's”. Becomings and the secret: “cf. the secret as the invention of the war-machine, as opposed to the 'publicity' of the despot or the man of the State”. Dumézil's eccentric warrior, de Heusch's war machine « from outside », and Clastres's war against the State all appear, with « Genghis Khan and the emperor of China » and Moses's organization of flight. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 141-42 (PDF 156-57).
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Develops** (ATP 361-362). The plateau's sentence on figures and « the affections that happen to them » is worked through as a Euclid-Archimedes opposition: “What is the difference between a problem and a theorem? It's that a problem is not of the essence type; it is of the event type, something happening, or of the operation type.” The operations are listed as “an ablation, an addition, a squaring, a cubing (cubature), a whole surgery of the figure”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 194.
- **Confirms a gloss** (ATP 362). Deleuze's own gloss on *pro-jection* and *problemata*: “projective, this is problematist; pro-blem equals pro-jection. Literally, it's… it's… it's the same word, one in Latin, the other in Greek”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196.
- **Develops** (ATP 362). The note on Bouligand and the intuitionists names a school (Brouwer, Heyting, Griss, Bouligand) that “arose in reaction against the axiomatic”, with the Destouches couple as “a kind of epistemological domestic scene”. The seminar states the political stake the note leaves out: “You feel that this is our only chance in politics, if our comparison is founded with the axiomatic. How to get out of an axiomatic?” This ties minor science to the axiomatic of capital in Proposition XIV. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 192-93 (on ATP n. 21).
- **Develops** (ATP 362-363). The note's compressed « trans-intuition (continuity) » is explained through Poncelet's axiom of continuity: a secant, then a tangent, then a line outside the circle, with “two fictitious points” kept across the three cases. “You do not go beyond space toward a symbolic power; you are creating connectors of space.” A Cauchy-Poncelet polemic renews “the same opposition as the one we have just seen among the Greeks, between a Euclidean current and an Archimedean current”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196-97 (on ATP n. 23).
- **Develops** (ATP 362-370). Monge's “a theory of particular affects”: “he distinguishes the particular affects of bodies from general properties”. This stands behind the *affections* and *affectif* of the problematic pole (361-62) and the Monge of n. 36, whose « physique particulière » treats the « affections variables des corps ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196 (on ATP n. 23 and n. 36).
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.

The note's compressed « trans-intuition (continuity) » is explained through Poncelet's axiom of continuity: a secant, then a tangent, then a line outside the circle, with “two fictitious points” kept across the three cases. “You do not go beyond space toward a symbolic power; you are creating connectors of space.” A Cauchy-Poncelet polemic renews “the same opposition as the one we have just seen among the Greeks, between a Euclidean current and an Archimedean current”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196-97 (on ATP n. 23).

This stands behind the *affections* and *affectif* of the problematic pole (361-62) and the Monge of n. 36, whose « physique particulière » treats the « affections variables des corps ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196 (on ATP n. 23 and n. 36).
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.
- **Develops** (ATP 363). Wittfogel's *Oriental Despotism*, a parenthesis in plateau 12, is presented at length, with the despot as “the entrepreneur of large-scale works, starting with hydraulic works”. Plateau 13 (Proposition XII) carries the argument. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 26-30.
- **Develops** (ATP 364-365). Desargues “he was in contact with stonemasons. You see why, in this second conception, [contact] with stonemasons and stone cutting is quite important? Why? Because stone cutting really belongs to the "what's going on?" type. Obviously, stone cutting is problematic.” His condemnation « in Parliament » is noted on the same page. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 195.
- **Source** (ATP 364-366). Experiment as “réajustements, approximations  successives, recherche vers des formes nouvelles et non bonne forme à produire et reproduire” ("readjustments, successive approximations, search toward new forms and not a good form to produce and reproduce"), the source of the « approximations successives » at 364; Trudaine's assemblies: “l'assemblée générale qui réunit ceux de ses membres qui le veulent, le dimanche, en plus, au domicile de Trudaine, ne s'intéresse qu'aux ponts” ("on Sundays, at Trudaine's home, cares only about bridges"). *Source:* `cerfi/querrien_devenir_fonctionnaire.txt`, text-file ll. 172-73.
- **Source** (ATP 364-368). The origin of *taille par panneaux* against *équarrissage*: the Jesuits replace “à la taille des pierres par équarrissement qui suppose le va et vient du trait et de la main, la taille de la pierre par la tête, ils substituent la taille par panneaux qui suppose l'opposition du dessinateur  et de l'exécutant” ("cutting by squaring, which supposes the back-and-forth of the line and the hand … cutting by templates, which supposes the opposition of draftsman and executant"). D&G drop the Jesuits. *Source:* `cerfi/querrien_devenir_fonctionnaire.txt`, text-file l. 167.
- **Context** (ATP 364-368). Querrien writes out of the CERFI's *Recherches* 13, « Les équipements du pouvoir », with Deleuze, Guattari, and Foucault in its conversations. Chapter V, « le Discours du Plan », works through the French planning reports (1947 Plan de Modernisation et d'Equipement; the sixth Plan's *équipements collectifs*): the third sense of *plan* at 368. In one conversation capital already tears workers from “leurs petits ateliers, leur petit compagnonage, leur petite famille”. Querrien's study was paid for by a DGRST contract, the same State research money. *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), ch. V, pp. 195-200; p. 133.

The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. - **Develops** (ATP 362-370). Monge's “a theory of particular affects”: “he distinguishes the particular affects of bodies from general properties”. This stands behind the *affections* and *affectif* of the problematic pole (361-62) and the Monge of n. 36, whose « physique particulière » treats the « affections variables des corps ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196 (on ATP n. 23 and n. 36).
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.
- **Develops** (ATP 364-365). Desargues “he was in contact with stonemasons. You see why, in this second conception, [contact] with stonemasons and stone cutting is quite important? Why? Because stone cutting really belongs to the "what's going on?" type. Obviously, stone cutting is problematic.” His condemnation « in Parliament » is noted on the same page. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 195.
- **Source** (ATP 364-366). Experiment as “réajustements, approximations  successives, recherche vers des formes nouvelles et non bonne forme à produire et reproduire” ("readjustments, successive approximations, search toward new forms and not a good form to produce and reproduce"), the source of the « approximations successives » at 364; Trudaine's assemblies: “l'assemblée générale qui réunit ceux de ses membres qui le veulent, le dimanche, en plus, au domicile de Trudaine, ne s'intéresse qu'aux ponts” ("on Sundays, at Trudaine's home, cares only about bridges"). *Source:* `cerfi/querrien_devenir_fonctionnaire.txt`, text-file ll. 172-73.
- **Source** (ATP 364-368). The origin of *taille par panneaux* against *équarrissage*: the Jesuits replace “à la taille des pierres par équarrissement qui suppose le va et vient du trait et de la main, la taille de la pierre par la tête, ils substituent la taille par panneaux qui suppose l'opposition du dessinateur  et de l'exécutant” ("cutting by squaring, which supposes the back-and-forth of the line and the hand … cutting by templates, which supposes the opposition of draftsman and executant"). D&G drop the Jesuits. *Source:* `cerfi/querrien_devenir_fonctionnaire.txt`, text-file l. 167.
- **Context** (ATP 364-368). Querrien writes out of the CERFI's *Recherches* 13, « Les équipements du pouvoir », with Deleuze, Guattari, and Foucault in its conversations. Chapter V, « le Discours du Plan », works through the French planning reports (1947 Plan de Modernisation et d'Equipement; the sixth Plan's *équipements collectifs*): the third sense of *plan* at 368. In one conversation capital already tears workers from “leurs petits ateliers, leur petit compagnonage, leur petite famille”. Querrien's study was paid for by a DGRST contract, the same State research money. *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), ch. V, pp. 195-200; p. 133.

- **Earlier version** (ATP 367-368). The Husserl paragraph is first worked out here, with the step the plateau compresses: “The vague essence is roundness [le rond]. Roundness as what? Roundness as corporeality.” Roundness “roundness is simply the result, or the passage to the limit, of the process of rounding [arrondir]”, “this will be roundness as Archimedes conceives it via passage to the limit, in opposition to the circle as Euclid conceives it by essential definition”. On *vague*: “He adds, sublime word, these are vague essences. He knows very well that "vague" is vague. These are vagabond essences.” The session also gives roundness an affect: “It's not negative, it's something which already implies the operation of the hand, and constant correction”. *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 7-8.

Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Develops** (ATP 362-370). Monge's “a theory of particular affects”: “he distinguishes the particular affects of bodies from general properties”. This stands behind the *affections* and *affectif* of the problematic pole (361-62) and the Monge of n. 36, whose « physique particulière » treats the « affections variables des corps ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196 (on ATP n. 23 and n. 36).
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.
- **Source** (ATP 364-368). The origin of *taille par panneaux* against *équarrissage*: the Jesuits replace “à la taille des pierres par équarrissement qui suppose le va et vient du trait et de la main, la taille de la pierre par la tête, ils substituent la taille par panneaux qui suppose l'opposition du dessinateur  et de l'exécutant” ("cutting by squaring, which supposes the back-and-forth of the line and the hand … cutting by templates, which supposes the opposition of draftsman and executant"). D&G drop the Jesuits. *Source:* `cerfi/querrien_devenir_fonctionnaire.txt`, text-file l. 167.
- **Context** (ATP 364-368). Querrien writes out of the CERFI's *Recherches* 13, « Les équipements du pouvoir », with Deleuze, Guattari, and Foucault in its conversations. Chapter V, « le Discours du Plan », works through the French planning reports (1947 Plan de Modernisation et d'Equipement; the sixth Plan's *équipements collectifs*): the third sense of *plan* at 368. In one conversation capital already tears workers from “leurs petits ateliers, leur petit compagnonage, leur petite famille”. Querrien's study was paid for by a DGRST contract, the same State research money. *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), ch. V, pp. 195-200; p. 133.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 367-368). The Husserl paragraph is first worked out here, with the step the plateau compresses: “The vague essence is roundness [le rond]. Roundness as what? Roundness as corporeality.” Roundness “roundness is simply the result, or the passage to the limit, of the process of rounding [arrondir]”, “this will be roundness as Archimedes conceives it via passage to the limit, in opposition to the circle as Euclid conceives it by essential definition”. On *vague*: “He adds, sublime word, these are vague essences. He knows very well that "vague" is vague. These are vagabond essences.” The session also gives roundness an affect: “It's not negative, it's something which already implies the operation of the hand, and constant correction”. *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 7-8.

- **Source** (ATP 368). The nomad journeymen and the architect off site: “au départ les maçons sont nomades, se déplacent de chantier en chantier à travers  l'Europe” ("at first the masons are nomads, moving from site to site across Europe"); with the fourteenth century comes “le personnage du maître d’oeuvre,	de celui qui dessine et qui ordonne, dans un lieu séparé du reste du chantier” ("the figure of the master builder, who draws and orders, in a place separated from the rest of the site"). *Source:* `cerfi/querrien_devenir_fonctionnaire.txt`, text-file l. 162.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 368-369). The two planes (the compagnon's plan on the ground, the architect's on paper, 368) and the *heccéités* (369) are worked out together: “On the plane of composition, all we have at the moment are speeds and slownesses, and affects. No form. No subject.” Haecceity is brought in because “We need a word to help keep us from confusing it for a subject's individuality.” The planes are not good and bad: “There's a power balance between both planes.” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-II-Two-Forms-of-Segm/ATP-II-01-Feb-15-1977-English-4.txt`, seminar ATP II, 15 Feb 1977, PDF 8-10.

- **Earlier version** (ATP 369). Simondon's critique of hylomorphism through its example: “We could almost say that the technological model for this operation is: mold-clay.” The pair of singularities and traits of expression is defined, “matter as bearer of singularities, as bearer of affective qualities or traits of expression”, with the definition of an assemblage that the plateau brings in only at Proposition VIII: “And an assemblage is precisely a set of material singularities converging on a small number of well-determined traits of expression.” Footnote 33's « schème dynamique » is Simondon's modulation: “A modulator is a mold which constantly changes the measuring grid that it imposes [atteinte].” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 7-10.

## 12¶43 (Minnesota 369)
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Develops** (ATP 362-370). Monge's “a theory of particular affects”: “he distinguishes the particular affects of bodies from general properties”. This stands behind the *affections* and *affectif* of the problematic pole (361-62) and the Monge of n. 36, whose « physique particulière » treats the « affections variables des corps ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196 (on ATP n. 23 and n. 36).
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 368-369). The two planes (the compagnon's plan on the ground, the architect's on paper, 368) and the *heccéités* (369) are worked out together: “On the plane of composition, all we have at the moment are speeds and slownesses, and affects. No form. No subject.” Haecceity is brought in because “We need a word to help keep us from confusing it for a subject's individuality.” The planes are not good and bad: “There's a power balance between both planes.” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-II-Two-Forms-of-Segm/ATP-II-01-Feb-15-1977-English-4.txt`, seminar ATP II, 15 Feb 1977, PDF 8-10.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 369). Simondon's critique of hylomorphism through its example: “We could almost say that the technological model for this operation is: mold-clay.” The pair of singularities and traits of expression is defined, “matter as bearer of singularities, as bearer of affective qualities or traits of expression”, with the definition of an assemblage that the plateau brings in only at Proposition VIII: “And an assemblage is precisely a set of material singularities converging on a small number of well-determined traits of expression.” Footnote 33's « schème dynamique » is Simondon's modulation: “A modulator is a mold which constantly changes the measuring grid that it imposes [atteinte].” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 7-10.

## 12¶47 (Minnesota 370-371)
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Develops** (ATP 362-370). Monge's “a theory of particular affects”: “he distinguishes the particular affects of bodies from general properties”. This stands behind the *affections* and *affectif* of the problematic pole (361-62) and the Monge of n. 36, whose « physique particulière » treats the « affections variables des corps ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, session 9, PDF 196 (on ATP n. 23 and n. 36).
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.
- **Source** (ATP 371). The parenthesis on Dumézil compresses a mystique of speed tied to rhythm, the word fought over at 363-64: “Speed (extreme rapidity, sudden appearances and disappearances, lightning raids, etc.) is that behavior, that "rhythm," most suited to the activity of violent, improvisational, creative societies.” Against it, “the ordered public religion that holds sway throughout the year, except for that brief period when the masked monsters are unleashed, demands a majestic gait and solemn rhythm”. D&G take the pair out of myth into physics (the swerve from the line of fall). *Source:* `capture/dumezil_mitra_varuna.txt`, Mitra-Varuna, ch. II, "Celeritas and Gravitas", PDF 40; PDF 94.

## 12¶49 (Minnesota 371-372)
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Later version** (ATP 362-371). Boulez's formula for smooth space, « on occupe l'espace sans le compter » (fn. 15, 362), becomes the title of Deleuze's 1986 essay on Boulez: « “occupying without counting” ». D&G's source is Boulez, Penser la musique aujourd'hui; the essay is a later reading of the same formula. *Source:* `home-fields/deleuze_boulez_proust_time.txt`, Deleuze, « Boulez, Proust and Time: 'Occupying without Counting' » (1986), PDF 1.
- **Source** (ATP 371). The parenthesis on Dumézil compresses a mystique of speed tied to rhythm, the word fought over at 363-64: “Speed (extreme rapidity, sudden appearances and disappearances, lightning raids, etc.) is that behavior, that "rhythm," most suited to the activity of violent, improvisational, creative societies.” Against it, “the ordered public religion that holds sway throughout the year, except for that brief period when the masked monsters are unleashed, demands a majestic gait and solemn rhythm”. D&G take the pair out of myth into physics (the swerve from the line of fall). *Source:* `capture/dumezil_mitra_varuna.txt`, Mitra-Varuna, ch. II, "Celeritas and Gravitas", PDF 40; PDF 94.
- **Develops** (ATP 372-374). A politics of science, the subject of 372-74 (royal science arresting the flows of nomad science): “axiomatics, at the start of the 20th century, in the first half of the 20th century, in mathematics, but also in physics, worked as a means of blocking, of stopping”. The example is the axiomatization of physics in France by de Broglie's students against indeterminism. As with Eisenhower and NASA, “it operates as a kind of symbolic reterritorialization”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 191.

## 12¶54 (Minnesota 373-374)
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Develops** (ATP 372-374). A politics of science, the subject of 372-74 (royal science arresting the flows of nomad science): “axiomatics, at the start of the 20th century, in the first half of the 20th century, in mathematics, but also in physics, worked as a means of blocking, of stopping”. The example is the axiomatization of physics in France by de Broglie's students against indeterminism. As with Eisenhower and NASA, “it operates as a kind of symbolic reterritorialization”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 191.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 374-376). Noology's thesis (374-76: the image of thought as « the State-form developed in thought ») three years earlier: “For thought borrows its properly philosophical image from the state as beautiful, substantial or subjective interiority. It invents a properly spiritual State, as an absolute state”. And from the other side: “There is no State which does not need an image of thought which will serve as its axiomatic system or abstract machine, and to which it gives in return the strength to function”, « hence the inadequacy of the concept of ideology ». *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, p. 13 (PDF 28); ch. 3, p. 88 (PDF 103).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 374-376). Noology's State-form in thought (374-76) is the image of thought of 1968: “Recognition is a sign of the celebration of monstrous nuptials, in which thought 'rediscovers' the State, rediscovers 'the Church' and rediscovers all the current values”. Kant and Hegel are « philosophical labourers » because their philosophy remained marked by this model of recognition (cf. Goethe and Hegel as « State thinkers », 355-56). *Source:* `core/deleuze_difference_repetition.txt`, Difference and Repetition, ch. 3, « The Image of Thought », p. 136 (Patton trans.).

Noology's thesis (374-76: the image of thought as « the State-form developed in thought ») three years earlier: “For thought borrows its properly philosophical image from the state as beautiful, substantial or subjective interiority. It invents a properly spiritual State, as an absolute state”. And from the other side: “There is no State which does not need an image of thought which will serve as its axiomatic system or abstract machine, and to which it gives in return the strength to function”, « hence the inadequacy of the concept of ideology ». *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, p. 13 (PDF 28); ch. 3, p. 88 (PDF 103).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 374-376). Noology's State-form in thought (374-76) is the image of thought of 1968: “Recognition is a sign of the celebration of monstrous nuptials, in which thought 'rediscovers' the State, rediscovers 'the Church' and rediscovers all the current values”. Kant and Hegel are « philosophical labourers » because their philosophy remained marked by this model of recognition (cf. Goethe and Hegel as « State thinkers », 355-56). *Source:* `core/deleuze_difference_repetition.txt`, Difference and Repetition, ch. 3, « The Image of Thought », p. 136 (Patton trans.).

- **Earlier version** (ATP 374-376). Noology's thesis (374-76: the image of thought as « the State-form developed in thought ») three years earlier: “For thought borrows its properly philosophical image from the state as beautiful, substantial or subjective interiority. It invents a properly spiritual State, as an absolute state”. And from the other side: “There is no State which does not need an image of thought which will serve as its axiomatic system or abstract machine, and to which it gives in return the strength to function”, « hence the inadequacy of the concept of ideology ». *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, p. 13 (PDF 28); ch. 3, p. 88 (PDF 103).

- **Earlier version** (ATP 377-379). The Artaud-Rivière exchange of 377-79, read first in 1968: “Riviere defended the image of an autonomous thinking function, endowed in principle with its own nature and will.” “The reader notes with astonishment that the more Riviere believes himself to be close to an understanding of Artaud, the further away he is”. Artaud's case reaches a thought process that “amounts to the complete destruction of that image”. *Source:* `core/deleuze_difference_repetition.txt`, Difference and Repetition, ch. 3, pp. 146-47 (Patton trans.).

## 12¶67 (Minnesota 379-380)
- **Develops** (ATP 351-380). The hypothesis of Axiom I and Axiom II, restated as a response: “if it is true that the nomads invented the war machine, it is in specific environments, in determinate conditions and directed precisely against State apparatuses; and that the war machine was a kind of riposte, of parade, with regard to the State apparatus”. Armies are kept apart from the start: the nomads did not invent armies. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 3.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 377-379). The Artaud-Rivière exchange of 377-79, read first in 1968: “Riviere defended the image of an autonomous thinking function, endowed in principle with its own nature and will.” “The reader notes with astonishment that the more Riviere believes himself to be close to an understanding of Artaud, the further away he is”. Artaud's case reaches a thought process that “amounts to the complete destruction of that image”. *Source:* `core/deleuze_difference_repetition.txt`, Difference and Repetition, ch. 3, pp. 146-47 (Patton trans.).
- **Later version** (ATP 380-381). The *nomos* as « the consistency of a fuzzy aggregate » (380) comes back in 1987 as Deleuze's account of experience: “experience is what could be called a fuzzy aggregate (ensemble flou). It’s a fuzzy aggregate, that is, a collection of disparates”. “A fuzzy aggregate, how does one define it? Uniquely through what occurs to it after. It’s an aggregate in which a series of consolidations is going to be produced.” Principles turn into secondary processes produced in such an aggregate, “processes of unification; there are processes of centralization; there are processes of totalization; there are processes of subjectification”. A student had asked for fuzzy set theory at the end of the 1979-80 seminar (PDF 286), and Deleuze agreed. *Source:* `purdue/Leibniz-and-the-Baroque/15a-GD-Leibniz28April1987-English-Revision-2024.txt`, Leibniz and the Baroque, 28 Apr 1987, PDF 11 (Purdue English trans.).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 380-381). The sedentary road that parcels out a closed space and the nomad trajectory that distributes people in an open one (380-81) were first a pair of distributions in univocal being, with the same note to Laroche: “Then there is a completely other distribution which must be called nomadic, a nomad nomos, without property, enclosure or measure.” “To fill a space, to be distributed within it, is very different from distributing the space.” *Source:* `core/deleuze_difference_repetition.txt`, Difference and Repetition, ch. 1, pp. 36-37 (Patton trans.).
- **Develops** (ATP 380-382). The migrant and the nomad of 380-82: “A migrant in its pure concept is someone who goes from one point to another, even if he does not know where he is going to stop.” “There are situated nomads (nomades sur place).” A body taken as a multiplicity occupying space « in a whirlwind fashion without counting it » can stay still. Toynbee's page (381) is credited: “it's the only very, very intelligent page that I have ever read on nomads”. Nomads are those who do not move when the desert grows. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 281-282.
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.

- **Context** (ATP 381). Kleist's « stationary process » and the trip in place (381), two months after the war machine sessions: “One sees very well what this process is in Kleist’s case: he jumps on horseback, and he crosses Germany.” “Let’s say it’s already the geographical sign of the process. There are people who stay in one place and are seized by the process.” The examples are Beckett's characters; the danger named is the process turning into a line of death. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-and-Other-Reflections/AO-05-27-80-English-2025-Update-2.txt`, Anti-Oedipus and Other Reflections, 27 May 1980, PDF 14 (Purdue English trans.).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 381). Speed without relative movement and the « voyages spirituels » made in place (381) start from Nietzsche's *Ecce Homo* in 1977: “You can be very fast without moving; you can go on incredibly fast trips while standing in place, and then be back before you’ve left.” Kleist is already the counterexample to Goethe: “Hölderlin and Kleist pay homage to Goethe, but that’s still just their pure hatred.” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-II-Two-Forms-of-Segm/ATP-II-02-March-8-1977-French-Update-1.txt`, seminar ATP II, 8 Mar 1977, PDF 9 (Purdue English trans.; the file so named holds the English).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 381-384). Speed against movement (381) and smooth space between forest and agriculture (384), in 1977: “Absolute speed can measure a rapid movement, but not a very slow movement or even an immobility, like a movement on the spot.” (as translated, the sentence denies what ATP affirms, « a speed may be very slow, or even immobile »; check the French of Dialogues). “Absolute speed is the speed of nomads, even when they move about slowly.” “The steppe always grows from the middle, it is between the great forests and the great empires.” *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, « A Conversation », p. 31 (PDF 46).

## 12¶71 (Minnesota 381)
- **Later version** (ATP 380-381). The *nomos* as « the consistency of a fuzzy aggregate » (380) comes back in 1987 as Deleuze's account of experience: “experience is what could be called a fuzzy aggregate (ensemble flou). It’s a fuzzy aggregate, that is, a collection of disparates”. “A fuzzy aggregate, how does one define it? Uniquely through what occurs to it after. It’s an aggregate in which a series of consolidations is going to be produced.” Principles turn into secondary processes produced in such an aggregate, “processes of unification; there are processes of centralization; there are processes of totalization; there are processes of subjectification”. A student had asked for fuzzy set theory at the end of the 1979-80 seminar (PDF 286), and Deleuze agreed. *Source:* `purdue/Leibniz-and-the-Baroque/15a-GD-Leibniz28April1987-English-Revision-2024.txt`, Leibniz and the Baroque, 28 Apr 1987, PDF 11 (Purdue English trans.).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 380-381). The sedentary road that parcels out a closed space and the nomad trajectory that distributes people in an open one (380-81) were first a pair of distributions in univocal being, with the same note to Laroche: “Then there is a completely other distribution which must be called nomadic, a nomad nomos, without property, enclosure or measure.” “To fill a space, to be distributed within it, is very different from distributing the space.” *Source:* `core/deleuze_difference_repetition.txt`, Difference and Repetition, ch. 1, pp. 36-37 (Patton trans.).
- **Develops** (ATP 380-382). The migrant and the nomad of 380-82: “A migrant in its pure concept is someone who goes from one point to another, even if he does not know where he is going to stop.” “There are situated nomads (nomades sur place).” A body taken as a multiplicity occupying space « in a whirlwind fashion without counting it » can stay still. Toynbee's page (381) is credited: “it's the only very, very intelligent page that I have ever read on nomads”. Nomads are those who do not move when the desert grows. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 281-282.
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Context** (ATP 381). Kleist's « stationary process » and the trip in place (381), two months after the war machine sessions: “One sees very well what this process is in Kleist’s case: he jumps on horseback, and he crosses Germany.” “Let’s say it’s already the geographical sign of the process. There are people who stay in one place and are seized by the process.” The examples are Beckett's characters; the danger named is the process turning into a line of death. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-and-Other-Reflections/AO-05-27-80-English-2025-Update-2.txt`, Anti-Oedipus and Other Reflections, 27 May 1980, PDF 14 (Purdue English trans.).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 381). Speed without relative movement and the « voyages spirituels » made in place (381) start from Nietzsche's *Ecce Homo* in 1977: “You can be very fast without moving; you can go on incredibly fast trips while standing in place, and then be back before you’ve left.” Kleist is already the counterexample to Goethe: “Hölderlin and Kleist pay homage to Goethe, but that’s still just their pure hatred.” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-II-Two-Forms-of-Segm/ATP-II-02-March-8-1977-French-Update-1.txt`, seminar ATP II, 8 Mar 1977, PDF 9 (Purdue English trans.; the file so named holds the English).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 381-384). Speed against movement (381) and smooth space between forest and agriculture (384), in 1977: “Absolute speed can measure a rapid movement, but not a very slow movement or even an immobility, like a movement on the spot.” (as translated, the sentence denies what ATP affirms, « a speed may be very slow, or even immobile »; check the French of Dialogues). “Absolute speed is the speed of nomads, even when they move about slowly.” “The steppe always grows from the middle, it is between the great forests and the great empires.” *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, « A Conversation », p. 31 (PDF 46).

Speed against movement (381) and smooth space between forest and agriculture (384), in 1977: “Absolute speed can measure a rapid movement, but not a very slow movement or even an immobility, like a movement on the spot.” (as translated, the sentence denies what ATP affirms, « a speed may be very slow, or even immobile »; check the French of Dialogues). “Absolute speed is the speed of nomads, even when they move about slowly.” “The steppe always grows from the middle, it is between the great forests and the great empires.” *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, « A Conversation », p. 31 (PDF 46).
- **Source** (ATP 383-384). D&G's « formule de Georges Bataille » (383) is a section heading: « “Nascent Islam or Society Reduced to Military Enterprise” ». - **Source** (ATP 384). The prophetism that n. 58 (384) says « can be generalized » to religion turned war machine: a society threatened by its chiefs, Clastres writes, “it responded by calling up from within itself and releasing forces capable” of thwarting them; the prophets managed “to unify, in the religious migration, the multifarious variety of the tribes”, and “the insurrectional act of the prophets against the chiefs conferred on the former, through a strange” reversal, more power than the chiefs. Clastres himself warns that beneath the prophet “the silent figure of the Despot may be hiding”, which D&G's turning of the absolute State against the State-form leaves aside. *Source:* `core/clastres_society_against_the_state.txt`, Clastres, Society against the State, ch. 11 (Hurley trans.), pp. 215-16 (PDF 216-17).

Clastres himself warns that beneath the prophet “the silent figure of the Despot may be hiding”, which D&G's turning of the absolute State against the State-form leaves aside. *Source:* `core/clastres_society_against_the_state.txt`, Clastres, Society against the State, ch. 11 (Hurley trans.), pp. 215-16 (PDF 216-17).

## 12¶80 (Minnesota 384-385)
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 381-384). Speed against movement (381) and smooth space between forest and agriculture (384), in 1977: “Absolute speed can measure a rapid movement, but not a very slow movement or even an immobility, like a movement on the spot.” (as translated, the sentence denies what ATP affirms, « a speed may be very slow, or even immobile »; check the French of Dialogues). “Absolute speed is the speed of nomads, even when they move about slowly.” “The steppe always grows from the middle, it is between the great forests and the great empires.” *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, « A Conversation », p. 31 (PDF 46).
- **Source** (ATP 383-384). D&G's « formule de Georges Bataille » (383) is a section heading: « “Nascent Islam or Society Reduced to Military Enterprise” ». Bataille sets it in his part on « The Society of Military Enterprise and the Society of Religious Enterprise », where “these pre-Islamic Arabs had not reached the stage of military enterprise any more than the Aztecs had”. *Source:* `collapse-readings/The_Accursed_Share__Volume_1__Consumption.txt`, Bataille, The Accursed Share, vol. 1, part III, ch. 2 (Hurley trans.), PDF 74.
- **Source** (ATP 384). The prophetism that n. 58 (384) says « can be generalized » to religion turned war machine: a society threatened by its chiefs, Clastres writes, “it responded by calling up from within itself and releasing forces capable” of thwarting them; the prophets managed “to unify, in the religious migration, the multifarious variety of the tribes”, and “the insurrectional act of the prophets against the chiefs conferred on the former, through a strange” reversal, more power than the chiefs. Clastres himself warns that beneath the prophet “the silent figure of the Despot may be hiding”, which D&G's turning of the absolute State against the State-form leaves aside. *Source:* `core/clastres_society_against_the_state.txt`, Clastres, Society against the State, ch. 11 (Hurley trans.), pp. 215-16 (PDF 216-17).
- **Context** (ATP 385-386). Virilio's State as *voirie* and the State as « échangeur routier » (385-86) have a CERFI precedent in the road as State equipment: « « La route comme pièce dans un « aménagement du territoire » : ou, d'une façon plus resserrée encore, l'autoroute qui « consomme >> elle-même les voitures dont elle assure la production. » » (« The road as a piece in a 'planning of the territory': or, more tightly still, the highway that 'consumes' the very cars whose production it ensures. ») *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), « Généalogie du capital 1. Les équipements du pouvoir », p. 185.
- **Develops** (ATP 385-386). The State's task « to striate the space over which it reigns » and Virilio's *voirie* (385-86) in class, credited to « Virilio's studies » from earlier years: “the fundamental act of the State is the establishment, or the planning [aménagement] of the territory.” That is, “it is the superposition onto the territorialities of lineages, onto territorialities-lineages, of a geometrical space. It is the geometrical reason of the State.” The word is the CERFI's and the French Plan's *aménagement du territoire* (cf. 12¶36, 12¶41). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 65.

## 12¶87 (Minnesota 387)

- **Source** (ATP 387). Jethro's counsel (387) in Exodus is decimal but judicial: “place such over them, to be rulers of thousands, and rulers of hundreds, rulers of fifties, and rulers of tens: 18:22 And let them judge the people at all seasons”. The military census comes later, in Numbers (src-numbers). « Le Qénien » is from Judges 1:16, “the children of the Kenite, Moses’ father in law”; Exodus calls him the priest of Midian. *Source:* `home-fields/kjv_gutenberg.txt`, Exodus 18:21-22; Judges 1:16 (KJV).

## 12¶89 (Minnesota 387-388)
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). - **Source** (ATP 387). Jethro's counsel (387) in Exodus is decimal but judicial: “place such over them, to be rulers of thousands, and rulers of hundreds, rulers of fifties, and rulers of tens: 18:22 And let them judge the people at all seasons”. The military census comes later, in Numbers (src-numbers). « Le Qénien » is from Judges 1:16, “the children of the Kenite, Moses’ father in law”; Exodus calls him the priest of Midian. *Source:* `home-fields/kjv_gutenberg.txt`, Exodus 18:21-22; Judges 1:16 (KJV).
- **Develops** (ATP 388). The source of the numbering number lies in « the need for marching orders and displacements in the desert »: “In the famous Book of Numbers, Moses inherits numerical organization from the nomads.” “As if by chance he finds himself, in a sense without having wanted it (which is curious in itself), more or less despite himself, in the position of having constituted a war machine.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 5.
- **Source** (ATP 388-389). The numbering number of Proposition VI (388-89) is a census for war in the desert: “Take ye the sum of all the congregation of the children of Israel, after their families, by the house of their fathers, with the number of their names, every male by their polls; 1:3 From twenty years old and upward, all that are able to go forth to war in Israel: thou and Aaron shall number them by their armies.” *Source:* `home-fields/kjv_gutenberg.txt`, Numbers 1:2-3 (KJV).

The numbering number of Proposition VI (388-89) is a census for war in the desert: “Take ye the sum of all the congregation of the children of Israel, after their families, by the house of their fathers, with the number of their names, every male by their polls; 1:3 From twenty years old and upward, all that are able to go forth to war in Israel: thou and Aaron shall number them by their armies.” *Source:* `home-fields/kjv_gutenberg.txt`, Numbers 1:2-3 (KJV).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 390-398). Proposition VII's assemblage before the tool, and the man-horse-bow unit of 391, come from the 1973 appendix to Anti-Oedipus: “The human-horsebow ensemble forms a nomadic war machine under the conditions of the steppe. Men form a labor machine under the bureaucratic conditions of the great empires.” “One and the same thing can be a tool or a machine, according to whether the "machinic phylum" takes hold of it or not, passes or does not pass through it.” The hoplite and the phalanx are already the example; and “there are always machines that precede tools, always phyla that determine at a given moment which tools, which men will enter as machine components”. ATP drops the « desiring-machine » and keeps the phylum and the assemblage. *Source:* `cerfi/guattari_chaosophy.txt`, Deleuze and Guattari, « Balance-Sheet for Desiring-Machines » (1973), in Chaosophy, PDF 89-91.

## 12¶97 (Minnesota 390-391)
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 390-398). Proposition VII's assemblage before the tool, and the man-horse-bow unit of 391, come from the 1973 appendix to Anti-Oedipus: “The human-horsebow ensemble forms a nomadic war machine under the conditions of the steppe. Men form a labor machine under the bureaucratic conditions of the great empires.” “One and the same thing can be a tool or a machine, according to whether the "machinic phylum" takes hold of it or not, passes or does not pass through it.” The hoplite and the phalanx are already the example; and “there are always machines that precede tools, always phyla that determine at a given moment which tools, which men will enter as machine components”. ATP drops the « desiring-machine » and keeps the phylum and the assemblage. *Source:* `cerfi/guattari_chaosophy.txt`, Deleuze and Guattari, « Balance-Sheet for Desiring-Machines » (1973), in Chaosophy, PDF 89-91.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 391-392). In 1973 Genghis Khan is the despot's dream: « “L'idéal du despotisme, c'est Gengis Khan : tout détruire (sauf les artisans). Mais, sans ville, il n'arrive pas à surcoder les territorialités primitives.” » (« The ideal of despotism is Genghis Khan: destroy everything (except the artisans). But, without a town, he does not manage to overcode the primitive territorialities. ») By 391-92 the same figure is the nomad who numbers the lineages and extracts his guard from them: the war machine has been split off from the despot. *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), discussion, p. 29 (turn marked « Félix »).
- **Context** (ATP 391-399). The CERFI already sets the Roman legion (391) against the feudal horseman (399, the stirrup): « “Ce morcellement territorial est le fait d'une machine militaire, le guerrier à cheval, maître du sol” » (« This territorial parceling is the work of a military machine, the mounted warrior, master of the soil »). « “Loin de déterritorialiser les flux de travailleurs, à la façon de la légion romaine, cette machine de guerre médiévale renvoie ainsi les paysans à la terre, et les y fixe.” » (« Far from deterritorializing the flows of workers, in the manner of the Roman legion, this medieval war machine sends the peasants back to the land, and fixes them there. ») *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), « Les équipements du pouvoir », p. 84.

Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 390-398). Proposition VII's assemblage before the tool, and the man-horse-bow unit of 391, come from the 1973 appendix to Anti-Oedipus: “The human-horsebow ensemble forms a nomadic war machine under the conditions of the steppe. Men form a labor machine under the bureaucratic conditions of the great empires.” “One and the same thing can be a tool or a machine, according to whether the "machinic phylum" takes hold of it or not, passes or does not pass through it.” The hoplite and the phalanx are already the example; and “there are always machines that precede tools, always phyla that determine at a given moment which tools, which men will enter as machine components”. ATP drops the « desiring-machine » and keeps the phylum and the assemblage. *Source:* `cerfi/guattari_chaosophy.txt`, Deleuze and Guattari, « Balance-Sheet for Desiring-Machines » (1973), in Chaosophy, PDF 89-91.
- **Context** (ATP 391-399). The CERFI already sets the Roman legion (391) against the feudal horseman (399, the stirrup): « “Ce morcellement territorial est le fait d'une machine militaire, le guerrier à cheval, maître du sol” » (« This territorial parceling is the work of a military machine, the mounted warrior, master of the soil »). « “Loin de déterritorialiser les flux de travailleurs, à la façon de la légion romaine, cette machine de guerre médiévale renvoie ainsi les paysans à la terre, et les y fixe.” » (« Far from deterritorializing the flows of workers, in the manner of the Roman legion, this medieval war machine sends the peasants back to the land, and fixes them there. ») *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), « Les équipements du pouvoir », p. 84.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 393-394). « Les nomades n'ont pas d'histoire, ils n'ont qu'une géographie » (393) is already in Dialogues: “The nomads have neither past nor future, they have only becomings, woman-becoming, animal-becoming, horse-becoming: their extraordinary animalist art. Nomads have no history, they only have geography.” ATP adds the reason: the nomads' defeat was so complete that history became one with the triumph of States. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 1, « A Conversation », p. 31 (PDF 46).

- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 390-398). Proposition VII's assemblage before the tool, and the man-horse-bow unit of 391, come from the 1973 appendix to Anti-Oedipus: “The human-horsebow ensemble forms a nomadic war machine under the conditions of the steppe. Men form a labor machine under the bureaucratic conditions of the great empires.” “One and the same thing can be a tool or a machine, according to whether the "machinic phylum" takes hold of it or not, passes or does not pass through it.” The hoplite and the phalanx are already the example; and “there are always machines that precede tools, always phyla that determine at a given moment which tools, which men will enter as machine components”. ATP drops the « desiring-machine » and keeps the phylum and the assemblage. *Source:* `cerfi/guattari_chaosophy.txt`, Deleuze and Guattari, « Balance-Sheet for Desiring-Machines » (1973), in Chaosophy, PDF 89-91.
- **Context** (ATP 391-399). The CERFI already sets the Roman legion (391) against the feudal horseman (399, the stirrup): « “Ce morcellement territorial est le fait d'une machine militaire, le guerrier à cheval, maître du sol” » (« This territorial parceling is the work of a military machine, the mounted warrior, master of the soil »). « “Loin de déterritorialiser les flux de travailleurs, à la façon de la légion romaine, cette machine de guerre médiévale renvoie ainsi les paysans à la terre, et les y fixe.” » (« Far from deterritorializing the flows of workers, in the manner of the Roman legion, this medieval war machine sends the peasants back to the land, and fixes them there. ») *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), « Les équipements du pouvoir », p. 84.
- **Develops** (ATP 395-396). The weapon and the tool cannot be told apart as objects: “A pitchfork, such as the peasant’s pitchfork, is it a weapon or a tool? It depends on what he does with it, eh?” The difference is the activity they are taken into. “This is why weapons are fundamentally projective”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 9.
- **Develops** (ATP 397-398). The two models of the motor (work, free action) are worked out from the physics of work: “it is the tool that is defined through labour”; “Force through displacements equals work. And work, what is that? It is irreducibly a social and physical concept.” The engineers of the nineteenth century stand « at the hinge point of the elaboration of the physical concept of work and the sociological concept of labour » (an allusion to Querrien's work on the Ponts et chaussées). Free action's model is “a vorticial [tourbillonnaire] movement that simultaneously occupies a maximum of points of space”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6-8.

The CERFI already sets the Roman legion (391) against the feudal horseman (399, the stirrup): « “Ce morcellement territorial est le fait d'une machine militaire, le guerrier à cheval, maître du sol” » (« This territorial parceling is the work of a military machine, the mounted warrior, master of the soil »). « “Loin de déterritorialiser les flux de travailleurs, à la façon de la légion romaine, cette machine de guerre médiévale renvoie ainsi les paysans à la terre, et les y fixe.” » (« Far from deterritorializing the flows of workers, in the manner of the Roman legion, this medieval war machine sends the peasants back to the land, and fixes them there. ») *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), « Les équipements du pouvoir », p. 84.
- **Develops** (ATP 397-398). The two models of the motor (work, free action) are worked out from the physics of work: “it is the tool that is defined through labour”; “Force through displacements equals work. And work, what is that? It is irreducibly a social and physical concept.” The engineers of the nineteenth century stand « at the hinge point of the elaboration of the physical concept of work and the sociological concept of labour » (an allusion to Querrien's work on the Ponts et chaussées). Free action's model is “a vorticial [tourbillonnaire] movement that simultaneously occupies a maximum of points of space”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6-8.

## 12¶114 (Minnesota 397-398)
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 390-398). Proposition VII's assemblage before the tool, and the man-horse-bow unit of 391, come from the 1973 appendix to Anti-Oedipus: “The human-horsebow ensemble forms a nomadic war machine under the conditions of the steppe. Men form a labor machine under the bureaucratic conditions of the great empires.” “One and the same thing can be a tool or a machine, according to whether the "machinic phylum" takes hold of it or not, passes or does not pass through it.” The hoplite and the phalanx are already the example; and “there are always machines that precede tools, always phyla that determine at a given moment which tools, which men will enter as machine components”. ATP drops the « desiring-machine » and keeps the phylum and the assemblage. *Source:* `cerfi/guattari_chaosophy.txt`, Deleuze and Guattari, « Balance-Sheet for Desiring-Machines » (1973), in Chaosophy, PDF 89-91.

- **Develops** (ATP 397-398). The two models of the motor (work, free action) are worked out from the physics of work: “it is the tool that is defined through labour”; “Force through displacements equals work.
