The CERFI already sets the Roman legion (391) against the feudal horseman (399, the stirrup): « “Ce morcellement territorial est le fait d'une machine militaire, le guerrier à cheval, maître du sol” » (« This territorial parceling is the work of a military machine, the mounted warrior, master of the soil »). « “Loin de déterritorialiser les flux de travailleurs, à la façon de la légion romaine, cette machine de guerre médiévale renvoie ainsi les paysans à la terre, et les y fixe.” » (« Far from deterritorializing the flows of workers, in the manner of the Roman legion, this medieval war machine sends the peasants back to the land, and fixes them there. ») *Source:* `recherches13/recherches13_read.txt`, Recherches 13 (Dec 1973), « Les équipements du pouvoir », p. 84.
- **Develops** (ATP 397-398). The two models of the motor (work, free action) are worked out from the physics of work: “it is the tool that is defined through labour”; “Force through displacements equals work. And work, what is that? It is irreducibly a social and physical concept.” The engineers of the nineteenth century stand « at the hinge point of the elaboration of the physical concept of work and the sociological concept of labour » (an allusion to Querrien's work on the Ponts et chaussées). Free action's model is “a vorticial [tourbillonnaire] movement that simultaneously occupies a maximum of points of space”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6-8.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 399-400). « Le désir n'a rien à voir avec une détermination naturelle ou spontanée, il n'y a de désir qu'agençant, agencé, machiné » (399) restates Dialogues against the objection of “a desire which would be natural and spontaneous reality. We say quite the opposite: desire only exists when assembled or machined.” (Massumi's « assembling, assembled, desire » drops « machiné »). The courtly assemblage of desire there includes horse and rider: “the Lady no less than the horse, the sleeping knight no less than the wandering quest for the G rail”, the « zoosexual Eros of the horseman » of 399. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, ch. 3, « Dead Psychoanalysis: Analyse » (PDF 111, 113).
- **Develops** (ATP 399-400). The two regimes of feeling: “the sentiments, which refer back simultaneously to the theme of a development, of a formation, and which were really the feelings circulating in the milieu of the State apparatus”; “the affects, which for their part, are the feelings proper to the war machine”. Kafka's page « on the two compassions, the two justices » and Dumézil's two justices are invoked for it. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 10.

## 12¶128 (Minnesota 403-404)
- **Develops** (ATP 380-403). Axiom II gives the war machine three aspects (space, number, affect). The seminar lays out five polar oppositions, point by point against the State: space (“the nomadic war machine develops in a space that one could call a smooth space”), composition (“the war machine moves towards an arithmetical composition of men”), activity (free action against work), expression (“The coupling ‘weapons-jewels’ of the war machine and the coupling ‘tools-signs’ of the State apparatus.”), and feeling (affects against sentiments). Each is stated as a pole, with the warning that concrete cases are always mixed. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 4-10.
- **Develops** (ATP 404-416). The machinic phylum and itinerance: “a single and same matter-movement into which State apparatuses and war machines are plugged”, crossed by itinerant metallurgists. The seminar adds an example the plateau does not have: “There is absolutely a phylum of petrol.” What happens when it « becomes maritime »? Holey space: “the space of mining is indeed a holed space”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 12.
- **Develops** (ATP 404-416). Griaule's Dogon smith (n. 89, 412) and the deserters of 405 come together in class: the smith is feared, but “it is not at all because he is impure, as some ethnologists try to explain.” He is double, a twin: “He necessarily has two heads: he has a head on the side of the nomads, and a head on the side of the sedentary peoples”, with « arrangements » between the two kinds of smiths behind the ancient arms trade. Against the deserter legend: “It's like the atomic bomb, you understand: to use the steel saber, and to remake, reproduce steel sabers, it is not enough to… to… leak the secret”; one needs « an entire infrastructure ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 284.

## 12¶136 (Minnesota 407-408)
- **Develops** (ATP 404-416). The machinic phylum and itinerance: “a single and same matter-movement into which State apparatuses and war machines are plugged”, crossed by itinerant metallurgists. The seminar adds an example the plateau does not have: “There is absolutely a phylum of petrol.” What happens when it « becomes maritime »? Holey space: “the space of mining is indeed a holed space”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 12.
- **Develops** (ATP 404-416). Griaule's Dogon smith (n. 89, 412) and the deserters of 405 come together in class: the smith is feared, but “it is not at all because he is impure, as some ethnologists try to explain.” He is double, a twin: “He necessarily has two heads: he has a head on the side of the nomads, and a head on the side of the sedentary peoples”, with « arrangements » between the two kinds of smiths behind the ancient arms trade. Against the deserter legend: “It's like the atomic bomb, you understand: to use the steel saber, and to remake, reproduce steel sabers, it is not enough to… to… leak the secret”; one needs « an entire infrastructure ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 284.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 408-412). Proposition VIII's matter-flow and Simondon (408-12) first appear as a phenomenology of metal: “Metal is what compels us to think matter, and it’s what compels us to think matter as continuous variation.” After Simondon's « the taking of form is not accomplished in a single, visible instant », metal is « the conductor of all matter ». “the ingot is the continuous variation of matter, it’s a block” The session opens with Richard Pinhas on metallic sonority (Berlioz, Wagner, Varèse) and ends on the smith and the musician as twins: “it’s because music and metallurgy find themselves obsessed by the same problem: namely that metallurgy puts matter into a state of continuous variation just as music is obsessed with putting sound into a state of continuous variation”. The recording breaks off there. *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 10-14.

Proposition VIII's matter-flow and Simondon (408-12) first appear as a phenomenology of metal: “Metal is what compels us to think matter, and it’s what compels us to think matter as continuous variation.” After Simondon's « the taking of form is not accomplished in a single, visible instant », metal is « the conductor of all matter ». “the ingot is the continuous variation of matter, it’s a block” The session opens with Richard Pinhas on metallic sonority (Berlioz, Wagner, Varèse) and ends on the smith and the musician as twins: “it’s because music and metallurgy find themselves obsessed by the same problem: namely that metallurgy puts matter into a state of continuous variation just as music is obsessed with putting sound into a state of continuous variation”. The recording breaks off there. *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 10-14.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 409-410). The itinerant who follows the matter-flow (409-10) is first Husserl's phenomenologist: “The phenomenologist is the wanderer, the blacksmith.” And: “We have defined a kind of nomos matter, or better yet a vagabond materiality.” *Source:* `purdue/A-Thousand-Plateaus-IV-The-State-Apparat/ATP-IV-1-27-Feb-1979-English-REV-2026-1.txt`, seminar ATP IV, 27 Feb 1979, PDF 10.
- **Later version** (ATP 411-415). The « Gothic line » and nomad art of 411 and 415, worked out a year after the plateau: “the Gothic line, or northern line, is an abstract line”. Abstract is opposed not to figurative but to organic, and the line is non-geometric. “The life of the abstract Gothic line is a non-organic life.” Like the path of the barbarians, “it begins with Scythian art”. Worringer's *Abstraction and Empathy* and *Form in Gothic* are the sources, and the hand is « animated by a foreign will ». *Source:* `purdue/Painting-and-the-Question-of-Concepts/3a-Painting-28-April-1981-English-Revised-2024-1.txt`, seminar Painting, 28 Apr 1981, PDF 21-23.
- **Develops** (ATP 411-415). The answer to a student's question about the Gypsies returns to Proposition VIII: “the question of metallurgical peoples, of their autonomy, of their relationship with other types of people, seems to me something fundamental”. The Beaker peoples, « racially very, very different » but with one marker in their tombs from Spain to Eastern Europe (414), and the Tuareg smiths (perhaps descended from Jewish colonies or Crusaders) are the examples. The ingot (411-12) is neither money nor commodity: “the ingot is… it is… the very form of stockpiling, with the possibility of taking back, melting, refashioning, with the idea of an infinite process”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 278-280.

- **Develops** (ATP 413-415). Three spaces: smooth, striated, holey. Against confusing smooth with homogeneous (370): “Homogeneous space is the abstract product or abstract representation of striated space.” Holey space is the metallurgist's (the gîte, the ore deposit): “The holes are not a lack, they are not an absence.” “The quintessential metallurgical dwelling is the hole.” Jules Bloch's « Que sais-je ? » on the Gypsies (sedentary or nomad only secondarily, cave-dwellers first), cave slums in Turkey, the forest-smith's buried hut, and the space of holes in Eisenstein's *Strike* (413) follow. The smith is a twin, with one head among the nomads and one among the sedentary (Griaule on the Dogon, 415). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 280-284.

- **Source** (ATP 417-418). Moses's spies and the people's doubt (417-18): “Send thou men, that they may search the land of Canaan”, one man from each tribe; and on their return, “We be not able to go up against the people; for they are stronger than we.” The war of conquest falls to Joshua. Context: s01-numbers (Moses finds himself « more or less despite himself » with a war machine). *Source:* `home-fields/kjv_gutenberg.txt`, Numbers 13:2, 13:31 (KJV).

## 12¶161 (Minnesota 418-419)
- **Source** (ATP 417-418). Moses's spies and the people's doubt (417-18): “Send thou men, that they may search the land of Canaan”, one man from each tribe; and on their return, “We be not able to go up against the people; for they are stronger than we.” The war of conquest falls to Joshua. Context: s01-numbers (Moses finds himself « more or less despite himself » with a war machine). *Source:* `home-fields/kjv_gutenberg.txt`, Numbers 13:2, 13:31 (KJV).
- **Develops** (ATP 419-420). The appropriation of the war machine (Proposition IX): “there is the mercenary pole and the army pole”, encasting against appropriating. The universal solution: “one must give them pieces of land [terres]. They will literally have to be territorialized.” It works less through the technical form than through « the distribution of territories, systems of taxes, codes of service ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 13-14.
- **Develops** (ATP 419-421). Clausewitz's three points (419-20), worked through in class: annihilating the adversary “is what Clausewitz calls not “total war”, but “absolute war””. The difference lies in the objective: “To overthrow the adversary can once again be taking a fortress, and then that's it. But war becomes total when the overthrow or annihilation bears on the entire enemy people and the entire habitat.” Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 290-291.

## 12¶164 (Minnesota 419-420)
- **Develops** (ATP 419-420). The appropriation of the war machine (Proposition IX): “there is the mercenary pole and the army pole”, encasting against appropriating. The universal solution: “one must give them pieces of land [terres]. They will literally have to be territorialized.” It works less through the technical form than through « the distribution of territories, systems of taxes, codes of service ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 13-14.
- **Develops** (ATP 419-421). Clausewitz's three points (419-20), worked through in class: annihilating the adversary “is what Clausewitz calls not “total war”, but “absolute war””. The difference lies in the objective: “To overthrow the adversary can once again be taking a fortress, and then that's it. But war becomes total when the overthrow or annihilation bears on the entire enemy people and the entire habitat.” Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 290-291.
- **Develops** (ATP 420-421). Clausewitz's aim and objective, the terms of Proposition IX (420-21), restated for use on capital. The limited political aim against the objective: “The objective of war is what he calls, on his own, absolute war; and the objective of war is defined in a very simple way, namely, the overthrow or annihilation of the adversary”. And “total war is different: it occurs when the objective becomes unlimited, namely when the adversary is identified with the whole of the enemy population”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 256-257.
- **Develops** (ATP 420-422). Section 4 of Proposition XIV (« Power ») begins from Proposition IX's aim and objective. Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism) as the objective becomes unlimited. After Hannah Arendt's *Origins of Totalitarianism* (which Deleuze applies to fascism, not totalitarianism): “What fundamentally defines fascism is the initiation of a movement that has no other end (fin) than the movement, that is, the unlimited objective.” After Virilio, “the fascist state is a suicidal state”. The war machine became autonomous in fascism, and Deleuze hypothesizes that this is why the Allies chose Stalin over Hitler. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 289-292.

The war machine became autonomous in fascism, and Deleuze hypothesizes that this is why the Allies chose Stalin over Hitler. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 289-292.

## 12¶166 (Minnesota 420-421)
- **Develops** (ATP 419-420). The appropriation of the war machine (Proposition IX): “there is the mercenary pole and the army pole”, encasting against appropriating. The universal solution: “one must give them pieces of land [terres]. They will literally have to be territorialized.” It works less through the technical form than through « the distribution of territories, systems of taxes, codes of service ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 13-14.
- **Develops** (ATP 419-421). Clausewitz's three points (419-20), worked through in class: annihilating the adversary “is what Clausewitz calls not “total war”, but “absolute war””. The difference lies in the objective: “To overthrow the adversary can once again be taking a fortress, and then that's it. But war becomes total when the overthrow or annihilation bears on the entire enemy people and the entire habitat.” Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 290-291.
- **Develops** (ATP 420-421). Clausewitz's aim and objective, the terms of Proposition IX (420-21), restated for use on capital. The limited political aim against the objective: “The objective of war is what he calls, on his own, absolute war; and the objective of war is defined in a very simple way, namely, the overthrow or annihilation of the adversary”. And “total war is different: it occurs when the objective becomes unlimited, namely when the adversary is identified with the whole of the enemy population”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 256-257.
- **Develops** (ATP 420-422). Section 4 of Proposition XIV (« Power ») begins from Proposition IX's aim and objective. Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism) as the objective becomes unlimited. After Hannah Arendt's *Origins of Totalitarianism* (which Deleuze applies to fascism, not totalitarianism): “What fundamentally defines fascism is the initiation of a movement that has no other end (fin) than the movement, that is, the unlimited objective.” After Virilio, “the fascist state is a suicidal state”. The war machine became autonomous in fascism, and Deleuze hypothesizes that this is why the Allies chose Stalin over Hitler. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 289-292.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 421-422). The « peace of terror » of 421-22 and 467 is already Virilio's: “Paul Virilio depicts the world State as it is sketched out today: a State of absolute peace still more terrifying than that of total war”, where a « secret capillarity » of black holes completes the « super-organized society ». *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », p. 139 (PDF 154).
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.

## 12¶168 (Minnesota 421-422)
- **Develops** (ATP 419-421). Clausewitz's three points (419-20), worked through in class: annihilating the adversary “is what Clausewitz calls not “total war”, but “absolute war””. The difference lies in the objective: “To overthrow the adversary can once again be taking a fortress, and then that's it. But war becomes total when the overthrow or annihilation bears on the entire enemy people and the entire habitat.” Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 290-291.
- **Develops** (ATP 420-421). Clausewitz's aim and objective, the terms of Proposition IX (420-21), restated for use on capital. The limited political aim against the objective: “The objective of war is what he calls, on his own, absolute war; and the objective of war is defined in a very simple way, namely, the overthrow or annihilation of the adversary”. And “total war is different: it occurs when the objective becomes unlimited, namely when the adversary is identified with the whole of the enemy population”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 256-257.
- **Develops** (ATP 420-422). Section 4 of Proposition XIV (« Power ») begins from Proposition IX's aim and objective. Total war arrives in three stages (Napoleon, 1914-18, fascism) as the objective becomes unlimited. After Hannah Arendt's *Origins of Totalitarianism* (which Deleuze applies to fascism, not totalitarianism): “What fundamentally defines fascism is the initiation of a movement that has no other end (fin) than the movement, that is, the unlimited objective.” After Virilio, “the fascist state is a suicidal state”. The war machine became autonomous in fascism, and Deleuze hypothesizes that this is why the Allies chose Stalin over Hitler. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 289-292.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 421-422). The « peace of terror » of 421-22 and 467 is already Virilio's: “Paul Virilio depicts the world State as it is sketched out today: a State of absolute peace still more terrifying than that of total war”, where a « secret capillarity » of black holes completes the « super-organized society ». *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », p. 139 (PDF 154).
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 422-423). The danger that Proposition IX closes on (the war machine turned to destruction, fascism, 422-23) is first a danger of lines of flight: “each time it is traced by a war-machine, the line of flight is converted into a line of abolition, of destruction of others and of itself”. “War is certainly not a metaphor.” Strindberg's « conjugal war-machine » and Fitzgerald's « alcoholic war-machine » are examples. On Kleist: “All Kleist's work rests on the following observation: there is no longer a war-machine on a grand scale like that of the Amazons”, with the question « how can one reinvent a new type of war machine ». *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 140-43 (PDF 155-58).
- **Earlier version** (ATP 422-423). The political question that Proposition IX and the conclusion of plateau 13 leave open, asked in the *Anti-Oedipus* years. Guattari: “It is evident that a revolution requires a war machine, but this is not a state apparatus.” Deleuze, taking it up: “revolutionary organization must be that of the war machine and not of the state apparatus, of an analyzer of desire and not an external synthesis”. *Source:* `cerfi/guattari_chaosophy.txt`, Chaosophy, interview with Deleuze and Guattari, early 1970s (PDF 41-44).
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
